01 / Orientation

Start Here

  1. Check the technology inventory and environment before choosing an output.
  2. Find the output in the asset dictionary. Follow upstream arrows in the data-flow view.
  3. Use the pipeline catalogue and evidence links to locate responsible definitions.
  4. Read control-flow conditions separately. A dependency does not establish data movement.
  5. Review coverage findings before making an operational decision.

Business purpose, ownership, SLAs, actual run success and recovery procedures require reviewed documentation or time-bound runtime evidence. This handbook does not infer them from names.

Recovered view / Existing evidence

Architecture overview

This view reconstructs the environment described by the supplied evidence. It does not prescribe a target architecture. Components, technology roles and boundaries are stated only where definitions or explicit metadata support them. Declared connections do not prove successful communication or running services.

Recovered view / Deployment and connection

Deployment & connection map

These arrows show configured deployment or connection references, separately from the data-flow and task-control diagrams. Groups use only found environments and boundary/project labels from supplied metadata. A Compose project label does not prove physical or security isolation. “Boundary not supplied” records missing evidence; it is not an invented application layer.

05 / Orchestration

Initial read-only checks

  1. Confirm environment, source service and collection time.
  2. Open the evidence location and compare its source hash.
  3. Check unresolved parameters and unsupported syntax findings.
  4. Request an authorized metadata or run export when runtime facts are needed.

Logs, validation & datasets

Dataset names identify declared resources; they are not a row sample. Logs and validation outputs are unknown unless explicitly provided as authorized evidence. Ask the owner where those records are retained. Do not infer a retry or recovery action from a diagram.

08 / Change awareness

Impact, snapshots & freshness

Use the CLI impact command to traverse supported downstream data or control relationships. Inspect each edge's evidence before interpreting the result. Use diff between stored snapshots to see added, removed and changed facts. Run status --config <reviewed-config.json> to recheck source hashes, registry and configuration against the latest snapshot. A saved page does not poll sources. Missing visibility can make impact or freshness checks incomplete.

Questions for the responsible owner

  • Which environment and service does each unresolved connection target?
  • Can reviewed purpose, ownership, SLAs and terminology be provided?
  • Which authorized export would resolve each unsupported or dynamic reference?
  • Where are the time-bound run records, logs and validation reports?

11 / Reviewed interpretation

Human context

Annotations come from the separate reviewed annotation file and survive regeneration. They are business interpretation, separate from parser observations.

12 / Vocabulary

Glossary

TermMeaning
ArtifactA file inventoried inside an authorized source scope.
DeclaredPresent in a static definition or configuration; not proof of execution.
ObservedCaptured through explicit time-bound runtime or metadata evidence.
InferredDerived from a supported rule; not directly observed.
UnknownInsufficient evidence or unsupported analysis.
Data flowA supported upstream-to-downstream data dependency.
Control flowA technology-specific dependency or condition between tasks.
EvidenceSanitized source location, source hash and parser/rule provenance.
NamespaceEnvironment-aware service/source context used to prevent identity collisions.

13 / Provenance

Evidence index

Source text is intentionally absent. Locate the authorized original through the relative location, then verify its hash and parser/rule version.
